I’ll know something needs doing, and I’ll sort of stare at it or put it off, but I don’t know if that’s because I’m physically tired or because I just can’t get myself to care about it the way I used to.\n\nSo… I don’t know. I wouldn’t want to say it wrong. I haven’t really noticed it in a clear way like that, like small tasks specifically wiping me out. It’s all kind of mixed together for me.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, have you found it harder to focus, make decisions, or think clearly lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to pin that down. I mean… I hear what you’re asking, and I don’t want to say yes just because I’ve been off lately, you know?\n\nI don’t know that I’ve noticed it as a clear thing, like, “Oh, I can’t focus” or “I can’t make decisions.” It’s more… everything feels kind of muddled in general, but I couldn’t tell you if that’s my thinking being slower or if I’m just not as interested or motivated. Like if I’m sitting with something in front of me, I might not move on it right away, but I don’t know if that’s because I can’t decide, or because I just don’t feel much pull to do anything with it.\n\nAt work, I suppose there are moments where I sit there longer than I should, but again, I don’t know if that’s focus exactly. I’m not walking around thinking, “My mind isn’t working.” It’s just… things don’t come as naturally as they used to, maybe. Or maybe I’m overthinking that now because you asked.\n\nSo I guess my honest answer is I’m not sure. I haven’t noticed it in a clean, obvious way where I could say yes, that’s definitely been happening.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, would you say that you’ve lost interest or pleasure in most activities, even ones you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably one of the clearer things, honestly. I don’t really look forward to much anymore.\n\nLike, I used to have things that felt like they broke up the week, you know? Bowling was one of them. It wasn’t just the bowling itself, it was seeing the guys, getting out of the house, having something on the calendar. And gardening too — I used to spend time out there and actually enjoy it, fussing with things, making sure the yard looked decent. It gave me some satisfaction. Now I’ll look at it and think, “I should go out there,” and then I just… don’t. I sit there and come up with some reason why not.\n\nSame with my wife. We used to go to gallery shows sometimes, or go dancing, and I knew she liked that. I liked it too, or at least I liked doing it with her. Now when something comes up, I find myself making excuses before I’ve even really thought about it. I’ll say I’m not up for it, or maybe another time, or it’s too much trouble to get ready and go out. But underneath that, it’s more like none of it feels worth the effort.\n\nAnd that sounds awful when I say it out loud, because these are things I used to care about. They weren’t chores. They were the parts of life that were supposed to feel good. But lately I just kind of sit there, and even when I know I should want to go, I don’t feel that pull anymore.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, have you noticed that you’ve been moving more slowly than usual, or that you can’t seem to sit still and feel restless a lot of the time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’ve noticed that, no… not in the way you’re describing it, exactly.\n\nI mean, I probably do move slower sometimes, but I couldn’t tell you if that’s actually me moving slower or just me taking longer to get around to doing things. Like, if I’m sitting in the chair and I need to get up and go do something, I might sit there for a while first, but that doesn’t feel like my body is stuck in slow motion or anything. It’s more like I just don’t quite get myself going.\n\nAnd restless… I don’t think so. I’m not pacing all over the house or tapping my foot constantly, at least not that I’ve paid attention to. My wife might notice something I don’t, I guess, but from my side of it, I wouldn’t say I feel like I can’t sit still. If anything, I probably sit too much. But again, I don’t want to make that into an answer if that’s not really what you mean.\n\nSo… I guess I’d say I’m not sure. I haven’t clearly noticed myself moving unusually slow, and I don’t really think of myself as restless lately.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, have you felt a loss of energy or noticed that you get tired much more easily than usual, even if you haven’t done much physically?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I can answer that cleanly. I mean… tired, sure, sometimes, but I don’t know if it’s more than what anybody would feel, or if I’m just noticing it because everything’s been kind of off.\n\nIt’s not like I’ve been doing heavy physical work and then getting wiped out right away. I’m not really sure I’ve noticed a specific change where I’d say, “Wow, I have no energy now.” Some days I don’t feel like getting up and doing things, but I don’t know if I’d call that being physically tired. It’s more like I don’t get started, if that makes sense. I can sit there thinking I should do something, and then a lot of time passes, but I’m not sure whether that’s exhaustion or just… not having much push in me.\n\nAnd I guess that’s where I get confused with these questions, because tired can mean a few different things. Like, am I sleepy? Am I worn out? Am I just not motivated? I don’t always know how to separate those. I don’t want to say yes and make it sound like I’m collapsing from fatigue, because I’m not sure that’s true. But I also don’t feel exactly normal, either.\n\nSo I guess the honest answer is I haven’t really noticed getting tired much more easily in a clear way. I just know I’m not doing as much, and I’m not always sure why.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, would you say that you feel depressed most of the time, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. I mean, that’s really why I came in, I think.\n\nMost days I wake up and it’s just there right away — this heavy, hollow feeling in my chest. Not always crying or anything dramatic like that, but it’s like something in me has been scooped out. I’ll be lying there before the day has even started, and I already feel weighed down by it.\n\nAnd the part that bothers me is I don’t feel like myself. I used to be, you know… I was the kind of guy who could talk to anybody. In sales, that was part of who I was, but it wasn’t just work. I could chat with people, joke around, enjoy my day in little ways. Now it feels like that version of me is gone, or at least I can’t reach him. I keep thinking, “Where did that Ruben go?” because I don’t feel like that man most days anymore.\n\nSo yes, depressed most of the time… that sounds right. Nearly every day, honestly. It’s not just a bad mood that passes after breakfast or after I get busy. It kind of follows me around.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight lately — like eating much less or much more than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I haven’t really been tracking it in any organized way, like weighing myself or paying close attention to meals.\n\nMy appetite… I don’t know. Some days food doesn’t sound very interesting, but I can’t say I’m eating much less in a clear way. And I don’t think I’ve suddenly started eating a lot more either. It’s more that I’ll sort of eat because it’s there, or because it’s time, not because I’m especially excited about it. But I don’t know if that counts as a change.\n\nAs far as weight, I haven’t noticed my clothes fitting very differently or anything like that. My wife hasn’t said, “You’re losing weight,” or “You’re gaining weight,” at least not that I remember. So… no, I don’t think there’s been a big appetite or weight change that I could point to. If there is, it hasn’t been obvious to me.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, would you say that you’ve completely lost interest in activities you used to enjoy, to the point where you don’t get any pleasure from them at all anymore?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, “completely” is a strong word, but if I’m being honest, it feels pretty close to that.\n\nI used to have things I actually looked forward to. Bowling, for example — that was one of those simple things where I’d see the guys, talk a little trash, have a laugh, get out of my own head for a while. And now when it comes up, I just… I find reasons not to go. I’ll tell myself I’m not up for it, or it’s too much trouble, or I’ll go next time. But underneath that, it’s more like I can’t imagine enjoying it, so why bother?\n\nSame with the garden. I used to like being out there, even just pulling weeds or messing with the plants. It made me feel useful, I guess. Now I look at it and it just feels like another thing sitting there waiting for me, and I don’t feel any pull toward it.\n\nAnd my wife and I used to go to gallery shows sometimes, or go dancing. I say maybe I’m not in the mood, or maybe we should do it another night. It’s not that those things became bad. It’s that they don’t feel worth the effort anymore.\n\nSo yes… I’m not getting much pleasure from the things I used to enjoy. Mostly I avoid them, and when I think about doing them, I don’t feel that spark I used to.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, would you say that you’ve completely lost interest in activities you used to enjoy, to the point where you don’t get any pleasure from them at all anymore?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I know I kind of said it already, but yes, that’s one of the clearest things for me.\n\nIt’s like all the things that used to feel normal and good now feel like chores before I even start them. Bowling used to be something I actually looked forward to — seeing the guys, getting out of the house, having something to laugh about. Now I’ll sit there thinking about going, and instead of any excitement I’m already coming up with reasons not to. I’ll tell myself I’m tired, or it’s too late, or I’ll go next week, but really it’s because I just don’t believe I’m going to enjoy it.\n\nThe garden is the same way. I used to like being out there, just working with my hands, watching things grow, feeling like I’d done something. Now I look at it and it’s just… effort. It doesn’t give me that feeling anymore.\n\nAnd with my wife, that’s the part that makes me feel the worst. We used to go to gallery shows, or go dancing sometimes, and those were things I should want to do with her. But now I just sit there and make excuses, like maybe another night, or I’m not up for it. None of it feels worth the effort, even though I know it used to matter to me.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, have you ever felt this way before — like you lost interest in things and felt down most days — or is this the first time you’ve experienced something like this?\n\nPatient: I don’t know how to answer that cleanly.\n\nI mean… I’ve had rough stretches before, sure. But whether it was like this — where things just feel flat and I’m not interested in anything — I’m not sure.\n\nI don’t remember it being this obvious before. But I can’t give you a clear yes or no about whether it’s happened before in the same way.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, have you ever had a period of time before when you felt down most days and lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say that clearly, honestly.\n\nMore than two weeks in a row… I mean, that’s the part I have trouble with. I’ve had times in my life where I was down, or not really myself, or where I stopped doing certain things for a while. But I don’t know if I ever sat there and thought, “This has been every day for two weeks,” you know? I wasn’t measuring it like that.\n\nI can remember being in funks before, where I’d get quiet, keep to myself, maybe not feel like going out. I don’t want to tell you yes if I’m only guessing.\n\nSo I guess the honest answer is: maybe, but I can’t really remember a clear period like that. I wouldn’t have been thinking, “I’ve lost interest in nearly everything for two weeks.” So I don’t want to say yes and make it sound like I know for sure. I’ll tell myself I’m tired, or it’s too late, or I’ll go next week, but really it’s because I just don’t believe I’m going to enjoy it.\n\nThe garden is the same way. I used to like being out there, just working with my hands, watching things grow, feeling like I’d done something. Now I look at it and it’s just… effort. It doesn’t give me that feeling anymore.\n\nAnd with my wife, that’s the part that makes me feel the worst. We used to go to gallery shows, or go dancing sometimes, and those were things I should want to do with her. But now I just sit there and make excuses, like maybe another night, or I’m not up for it. None of it feels worth the effort, even though I know it used to matter to me.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, have you ever felt this way before — like you lost interest in things and felt down most days — or is this the first time you’ve experienced something like this?\n\nPatient: I don’t know how to answer that cleanly.\n\nI mean… I’ve had rough stretches before, sure. I think most people have, especially by my age. Times where work was stressful, or I didn’t feel like myself, or I was in a bad mood for a while. But whether it was like this — where things just feel flat and I’m not interested in anything — I’m not sure.\n\nI don’t remember it being this obvious before. Or maybe I didn’t think of it the same way at the time. I’m not someone who’s kept track of my moods over the years, you know? I usually just told myself, “You’re tired,” or “You’re in a rut,” or “Just push through it.” So it’s hard for me to say whether this is the first time exactly, or if I’ve had smaller versions of it before and just didn’t pay attention.\n\nRight now it feels different enough that I notice it. But I can’t give you a clear yes or no about whether it’s happened before in the same way.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, have you ever had a period of time before when you felt down most days and lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say that clearly, honestly.\n\nMore than two weeks in a row… I mean, that’s the part I have trouble with. I’ve had times in my life where I was down, or not really myself, or where I stopped doing certain things for a while. But I don’t know if I ever sat there and thought, “This has been every day for two weeks,” you know? I wasn’t measuring it like that.\n\nI can remember being in funks before, where I’d get quiet, keep to myself, maybe not feel like going out. But whether I lost interest in everything the way I have now, and whether it lasted past two weeks… I just don’t know. I don’t want to tell you yes if I’m only guessing.\n\nSo I guess the honest answer is: maybe, but I can’t really remember a clear period like that. This time feels noticeable to me in a way that’s hard to ignore, but I can’t give you a clean yes or no about the past.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, have you ever had a time before when you felt down most days and lost interest in most things for two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: I know you’re asking it a little differently, and I’m trying not to dodge it, but I still don’t know that I can give you a solid answer.\n\nI’ve definitely had periods before where I was down and pulled back from things. I hear what you’re asking — even if I didn’t label it that way at the time — and I still don’t know that I can honestly say yes.

More than I want to admit, honestly.\n\nI keep catching myself thinking I’m just… useless now. That’s the word that comes up in my head. Useless. And I know if someone else said that to me, I’d probably tell them that’s not fair, that people go through things, that it doesn’t erase who they are. But when it’s me, I can’t seem to believe that.\n\nI’ll sit there and think about all those years I spent taking care of clients, keeping relationships going, being dependable, bringing money in, providing for my family… and it’s like none of it counts anymore because right now I can’t even handle the job the way I used to. I know it does. But that’s where my mind goes. Like I should be stronger than this, especially at my age, after doing this work for so long. I blame myself for not being able to just get it together. It makes me feel like I’ve failed, not just at work, but as the person I’m supposed to be.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, have you noticed that even small tasks seem to take a lot out of you lately, like you don’t have the energy to get through them?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean… I know I’m not doing great, but when you say energy, I don’t know if I can separate that from everything else.\n\nSome things do feel harder to start, I guess, but I don’t sit there measuring it like, “Oh, this task took all my energy.” It’s more like I look at things and I don’t feel much drive to do them, if that makes sense. I’ll know something needs doing, and I’ll sort of stare at it or put it off, but I don’t know if that’s because I’m physically tired or because I just can’t get myself to care about it the way I used to.\n\nSo… I don’t know. I wouldn’t want to say it wrong. I haven’t really noticed it in a clear way like that, like small tasks specifically wiping me out. It’s all kind of mixed together for me.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, have you found it harder to focus, make decisions, or think clearly lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to pin that down. I mean… I hear what you’re asking, and I don’t want to say yes just because I’ve been off lately, you know?\n\nI don’t know that I’ve noticed it as a clear thing, like, “Oh, I can’t focus” or “I can’t make decisions.” It’s more… everything feels kind of muddled in general, but I couldn’t tell you if that’s my thinking being slower or if I’m just not as interested or motivated. Like if I’m sitting with something in front of me, I might not move on it right away, but I don’t know if that’s because I can’t decide, or because I just don’t feel much pull to do anything with it.\n\nAt work, I suppose there are moments where I sit there longer than I should, but again, I don’t know if that’s focus exactly. I’m not walking around thinking, “My mind isn’t working.” It’s just… things don’t come as naturally as they used to, maybe. I haven’t noticed it in a clean, obvious way where I could say yes, that’s definitely been happening.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, would you say that you’ve lost interest or pleasure in most activities, even ones you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably one of the clearer things, honestly. I don’t really look forward to much anymore.\n\nLike, I used to have things that felt like they broke up the week, you know? Bowling was one of them. It wasn’t just the bowling itself, it was seeing the guys, getting out of the house, having something on the calendar. And gardening too — I used to spend time out there and actually enjoy it, fussing with things, making sure the yard looked decent. It gave me some satisfaction. Now I’ll look at it and think, “I should go out there,” and then I just… don’t. I sit there and come up with some reason why not.\n\nSame with my wife. We used to go to gallery shows sometimes, or go dancing, and I knew she liked that. I liked it too, or at least I liked doing it with her. Now when something comes up, I find myself making excuses before I’ve even really thought about it. I’ll say I’m not up for it, or maybe another time, or it’s too much trouble to get ready and go out. But underneath that, it’s more like none of it feels worth the effort.\n\nAnd that sounds awful when I say it out loud, because these are things I used to care about. They weren’t chores. They were the parts of life that were supposed to feel good. But lately I just kind of sit there, and even when I know I should want to go, I don’t feel that pull anymore.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, have you noticed that you’ve been moving more slowly than usual, or that you can’t seem to sit still and feel restless a lot of the time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’ve noticed that, no… not in the way you’re describing it, exactly.\n\nI mean, I probably do move slower sometimes, but I couldn’t tell you if that’s actually me moving slower or just me taking longer to get around to doing things. Like, if I’m sitting in the chair and I need to get up and go do something, I might sit there for a while first, but that doesn’t feel like my body is stuck in slow motion or anything. It’s more like I just don’t quite get myself going.\n\nAnd restless… I don’t think so. I’m not pacing all over the house or tapping my foot constantly, at least not that I’ve paid attention to. My wife might notice something I don’t, I guess, but from my side of it, I wouldn’t say I feel like I can’t sit still. If anything, I probably sit too much. But again, I don’t want to make that into an answer if that’s not really what you mean.\n\nSo… I guess I’d say I’m not sure. I haven’t clearly noticed myself moving unusually slow, and I don’t really think of myself as restless lately.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, have you felt a loss of energy or noticed that you get tired much more easily than usual, even if you haven’t done much physically?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I can answer that cleanly. I mean… tired, sure, sometimes, but I don’t know if it’s more than what anybody would feel, or if I’m just noticing it because everything’s been kind of off.\n\nIt’s not like I’ve been doing heavy physical work and then getting wiped out right away. I’m not really sure I’ve noticed a specific change where I’d say, “Wow, I have no energy now.” Some days I don’t feel like getting up and doing things, but I don’t know if I’d call that being physically tired. It’s more like I don’t get started, if that makes sense. I can sit there thinking I should do something, and then a lot of time passes, but I’m not sure whether that’s exhaustion or just… not having much push in me.\n\nAnd I guess that’s where I get confused with these questions, because tired can mean a few different things. Like, am I sleepy? Am I worn out? Am I just not motivated? I don’t always know how to separate those. I don’t want to say yes and make it sound like I’m collapsing from fatigue, because I’m not sure that’s true. But I also don’t feel exactly normal, either.\n\nSo I guess the honest answer is I haven’t really noticed getting tired much more easily in a clear way. I just know I’m not doing as much, and I’m not always sure why.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, would you say that you feel depressed most of the time, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. I mean, that’s really why I came in, I think.\n\nMost days I wake up and it’s just there right away — this heavy, hollow feeling in my chest. Not always crying or anything dramatic like that, but it’s like something in me has been scooped out. I’ll be lying there before the day has even started, and I already feel weighed down by it.\n\nAnd the part that bothers me is I don’t feel like myself. I used to be, you know… I was the kind of guy who could talk to anybody. In sales, that was part of who I was, but it wasn’t just work. I could chat with people, joke around, enjoy my day in little ways. Now it feels like that version of me is gone, or at least I can’t reach him. I keep thinking, “Where did that Ruben go?” because I don’t feel like that man most days anymore.\n\nSo yes, depressed most of the time… that sounds right. Nearly every day, honestly. It’s not just a bad mood that passes after breakfast or after I get busy. It kind of follows me around.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight lately — like eating much less or much more than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I haven’t really been tracking it in any organized way, like weighing myself or paying close attention to meals.\n\nMy appetite… I don’t know. Some days food doesn’t sound very interesting, but I can’t say I’m eating much less in a clear way. And I don’t think I’ve suddenly started eating a lot more either. It’s more that I’ll sort of eat because it’s there, or because it’s time, not because I’m especially excited about it. But I don’t know if that counts as a change.\n\nAs far as weight, I haven’t noticed my clothes fitting very differently or anything like that. My wife hasn’t said, “You’re losing weight,” or “You’re gaining weight,” at least not that I remember. So… no, I don’t think there’s been a big appetite or weight change that I could point to. If there is, it hasn’t been obvious to me.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, would you say that you’ve completely lost interest in activities you used to enjoy, to the point where you don’t get any pleasure from them at all anymore?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, “completely” is a strong word, but if I’m being honest, it feels pretty close to that.\n\nI used to have things I actually looked forward to. Bowling, for example — that was one of those simple things where I’d see the guys, talk a little trash, have a laugh, get out of my own head for a while. And now when it comes up, I just… I find reasons not to go. I’ll tell myself I’m not up for it, or it’s too much trouble, or I’ll go next time. But underneath that, it’s more like I can’t imagine enjoying it, so why bother?\n\nSame with the garden. I used to like being out there, even just pulling weeds or messing with the plants. It made me feel useful, I guess. Now I look at it and it just feels like another thing sitting there waiting for me, and I don’t feel any pull toward it.\n\nAnd my wife and I used to go to gallery shows sometimes, or go dancing. That sounds like something that should still be nice, right? Taking her out, getting dressed, being around people, seeing something interesting. But lately I just sit there and make excuses. I say maybe I’m not in the mood, or maybe we should do it another night. It’s not that those things became bad. It’s that they don’t feel worth the effort anymore.\n\nSo yes… I’m not getting much pleasure from the things I used to enjoy. Mostly I avoid them, and when I think about doing them, I don’t feel that spark I used to.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, would you say that you’ve completely lost interest in activities you used to enjoy, to the point where you don’t get any pleasure from them at all anymore?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I know I kind of said it already, but yes, that’s one of the clearest things for me.\n\nIt’s like all the things that used to feel normal and good now feel like chores before I even start them. Bowling used to be something I actually looked forward to — seeing the guys, getting out of the house, having something to laugh about. Now I’ll sit there thinking about going, and instead of any excitement I’m already coming up with reasons not to. I’ll tell myself I’m tired, or it’s too late, or I’ll go next week, but really it’s because I just don’t believe I’m going to enjoy it.\n\nThe garden is the same way. I used to like being out there, just working with my hands, watching things grow, feeling like I’d done something. Now I look at it and it’s just… effort. It doesn’t give me that feeling anymore.\n\nAnd with my wife, that’s the part that makes me feel the worst. We used to go to gallery shows, or go dancing sometimes, and those were things I should want to do with her. But now I just sit there and make excuses, like maybe another night, or I’m not up for it. None of it feels worth the effort, even though I know it used to matter to me.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, have you ever felt this way before — like you lost interest in things and felt down most days — or is this the first time you’ve experienced something like this?\n\nPatient: I don’t know how to answer that cleanly.\n\nI mean… I’ve had rough stretches before, sure. I usually just told myself, “You’re tired,” or “You’re in a rut,” or “Just push through it.” So it’s hard for me to say whether this is the first time exactly, or if I’ve had smaller versions of it before and just didn’t pay attention.\n\nRight now it feels different enough that I notice it. But I can’t give you a clear yes or no about whether it’s happened before in the same way.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, have you ever had a period of time before when you felt down most days and lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say that clearly, honestly.\n\nMore than two weeks in a row… I mean, that’s the part I have trouble with. I’ve had times in my life where I was down, or not really myself, or where I stopped doing certain things for a while. But I don’t know if I ever sat there and thought, “This has been every day for two weeks,” you know? I don’t want to tell you yes if I’m only guessing.\n\nSo I guess the honest answer is: maybe, but I can’t really remember a clear period like that. This time feels noticeable to me in a way that’s hard to ignore, but I can’t give you a clean yes or no about the past.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, have you ever had a time before when you felt down most days and lost interest in most things for two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: I know you’re asking it a little differently, and I’m trying not to dodge it, but I still don’t know that I can give you a solid answer.\n\nI’ve definitely had periods before where I was down and pulled back from things. I wouldn’t have been thinking, “I’ve lost interest in nearly everything for two weeks.” So I don’t want to say yes and make it sound like I know for sure. I know we’ve kind of gone around this a few times, and I’m sorry — I’m not trying to be slippery about it. I just don’t have a clean memory of that happening before.

But looking back, I can’t honestly pin down another time that clearly.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, have you had a period in the past where you felt down most days and lost interest in most things for two weeks or more, even if you didn’t think much of it at the time?\n\nPatient: I hear what you’re asking — even if I didn’t label it that way at the time — and I still don’t know that I can honestly say yes.\n\nLooking back, there were definitely times where I was more shut down, or where I wasn’t enjoying things as much, or I was kind of just getting through the day. But I can’t really separate out whether that was feeling down most days and losing interest in most things for two weeks or more, or whether it was just stress, being worn out, life stuff piling up… I don’t know.\n\nI’m not trying to be difficult. I just don’t have a clear memory where I can point to it and say, “Yes, that happened before for that long.” I may have brushed things off in the past, so it’s possible, but I don’t want to overstate it either.\n\nSo the best answer I can give is: I’m not sure. I can’t identify a definite past period like that.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, looking back, was there ever a time before this current period when you felt down most days and lost interest in most things for two weeks or more, even if you didn’t think much of it at the time?\n\nPatient: I know we’ve kind of gone around this a few times, and I’m sorry — I’m not trying to be slippery about it. I just don’t have a clean memory of that happening before.\n\nWhen I look back, I can think of times where I wasn’t great, where I was quieter or more withdrawn, maybe not as eager to do things. But I can’t honestly say, yes, there was a specific stretch before this where I was down most days and had lost interest in most things for two weeks or longer. I don’t know if it was that, or just stress, or being tired, or life getting heavy for a while.\n\nThis current time is the one that feels more obvious to me. Before that, it’s kind of blurry. So I guess the most truthful answer is: I don’t know. I can’t point to a definite earlier period like that."
